     6. C.   ORG      1.18.11 
WHEREAS, there may be in and may, hereafter from time to time, come into 
the hands of Mark S. Rajter , Treasurer of Troy School District, certain public 
monies belonging to or held for the State, County, or other political units of the 
State or otherwise held according to law, and 
WHEREAS, under the laws of Michigan, this Board is required to provide by 
Resolution for the deposit of all public monies, including tax monies coming into 
the hands of said Treasurer, on one or more banks, hereafter called bank(s) to 
be designated in such Resolution. 
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED  that said Treasurer, Mark S. Rajter , is 
hereby directed to deposit all public monies, including tax monies now in or 
coming into his hands as Treasurer in his name as Treasurer, into the followin g 
depository banks: 
Bank of America 
Bank of New York 
Charter One Bank 
Chase Bank 
Comerica Bank 
Fifth Third Bank 
MILAF 
PFM Securities, Inc. through Fifth Third Bank 
PNC Bank 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging to the District may be 
withdrawn by checks signed with a facsimile signature of the Treasurer, Mark S. 
Rajter, and in emergencies the handwritten signature of the Treasurer, Mark S. 
Rajter, or the Superintendent, Barbara A. Fowler, wh o may sign on the General 
Fund account and the Payroll account at Bank of America; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging to the District for the 
accounts established at PNC Bank  for purposes of paying Troy Career Center 
expenses may be withdrawn by checks with the handwritten signature of 
Dorothy Kaltz, Jennifer Llewellyn, Kathy Molesa; or Lyda Keough; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging to the District for the 
account established at Bank of America  for purposes of paying health care and 
dependent care claims may be withdrawn by checks signed with a facsimile 
signature of  the Treasurer, Mark S. Rajter , and for checks over $10,000, the 
additional handwritten signature of Jasen Witt , Assistant Superintendent for 
Human Resources or Monica Papasian, Finance Director; and 
10
     6. C.   ORG      1.18.11 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Treasurer, Mark S. Rajter , may open or 
close accounts with the above -listed institutions, provided it is in the interest of 
the District; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that funds of the District may be invested 
according to Board Policy D-1103; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Treasurer is authorized to combine 
money from more than one fund f or the purpose of making an investment 
authorized by subsection (1) (h) of Section 380.1223 of the Revised School 
Code; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the funds of the District may be invested with 
the following institutions: 
Ambassador Capital Management 
Bank of America 
Chase Bank 
Charter One Bank 
Citizens Bank 
Comerica Bank 
Fifth Third Bank 
Flagstar Bancorp 
MILAF 
Morgan Stanley Smith Barney 
PFM Securities, Inc. through Fifth Third Bank  
The Private Bank 
THE MAXIMUM AMOUNT TO BE INVESTED  with the above institutions is 
regulated by the school code. 
I, ______________________, Secretary of Troy School District, Oakland County, 
Michigan, certify that the Resolution was adopted by said Board at a meeting 
thereof held on the date specified above. 
Given under my hand and seal of the Troy School District in the County of 
Oakland in the State of Michigan this 18th day of January 2011. 
_______________________________  
Secretary, Troy Board of Education

      2. C.   01.18.11 
2 .   R E C O G N I T I O N        
C. Board of Education Recognition 
Each January the Troy School Distri ct recognizes and celebrates the 
work of a very impo rtant group of individuals: The Board of 
Education. Troy is truly fortunate to be governed by Board members 
who are tireless, professional, an d passionate about improving the 
opportunities of our st udents. The successes that  we receive in the 
Troy School District stem in large part from the support and guidance 
we receive from our Board member s. The Troy Board of Education 
works very hard to ensure that th e entire district is committed to 
promoting student achievement. Tonight, we wo uld like to recognize 
Board of Education members with a short video that honors their 
place and their many contributions to the rich history of the Troy 
School District.

BOARD WORKSHOP   MINUTES  DECEMBER 14, 2010 
    ADMINISTRATION BUILDING 
President Underwood called the workshop to order at 6:05 p.m.  In addition to M s. 
Underwood, the following Board members were present:  Mrs. Edmunds, Mrs. Fleming, 
Mr. Hauff,  Mr. Miletti, Ms. Philippart , and Mrs. Pochodylo .  Also in attendance were 
Superintendent Fowler  and administrators Birmingham, Keeling, Machesky, Stromayer , 
Witt, and Rajter.  The following items were discussed:   
1. Public Communication* 
2. Schools of Choice 105 & 105C Projections – Kindergarten and 1st Grade 
3. 2011/2012 Budget Assumption Discussion  
4. Other 
5. Adjournment 
The meeting was adjourned at 7:32 p.m. 
 Respectfully submitted, 
 Nancy L. Philippart, Secretary

 7. A. 01.18.11 
7. CURRICULUM 
A. CONSIDERATION:   Schools of Choice - Section 105 
In accordance with Michigan state law, school districts 
are required to pass a resolution each school year 
indicating whether or not t hey plan to implement a 
Schools of Choice program f or nonresident students who 
attend another district within their county.  It would be 
appropriate at this time for the Board of Education to 
consider adopting a resolution regarding Schools of 
Choice within Oakland County. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: __________________________ 
 Supported by: __________________________ 
be it therefore resolved that the Board of Education of the Troy 
School District, exercising the opt ion permitted by Section 105 of 
the State School Aid Act of 1979, as amended by Public Act 300 
of 1996, will accept applications for a limited Schools of Choice 
program for the 201 1-2012 school year for nonresident 
kindergarten and first-grade students within Oakland County. 
Ayes: __________ 
Nays: __________

 7. B. 01.18.11 
7. CURRICULUM 
B. CONSIDERATION:   Schools of Choice - Section 105c 
In accordance with Michigan  state law, school districts  are 
required to pass a resolution each school year indicating 
whether or not they plan to implement a Schools of Choice 
program for nonresident students who attend a district in 
another county, if that Intermediate School District is 
contiguous with the O akland Intermediate School District 
boundaries.  It would be appropriate at this time for the Board 
of Education to consider adopting a resolution regarding 
Schools of Choice, Section 105c.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: __________________________ 
 Supported by: __________________________ 
be it therefore resolved that the Board of Education of the Troy School 
District, exercising the option permitted by Section 105c  of the State 
School Aid Act of 1979, as amended by Public Act 300 of 1996, will 
accept applications for a limited Schools of Choice program  for th e 
2011-2012 school year for nonresident kindergarten and first -grade 
students who attend a school district in  another county if that 
Intermediate School District is contiguous with the Oakland Intermediate 
School District boundaries.

 7. C. 01.18.11 
7. CURRICULUM 
C. REPORT:   NASA DIME Competition 
DIME is a NASA competitive program that invites high school teams to 
develop an experiment concept, prepare a proposal for an experiment, 
and submit the proposal to NASA.   A NASA panel selects four top -
ranked proposals nationally.  These four teams will then continue their 
experiment development and fabrication leading to operation in the 
NASA drop tower in March , 2011.  NASA provides an expense -paid trip 
for each selected team to attend DIME Drop Days in March , 2011, at 
NASA’s Glenn Research Center in Cleveland, Ohio.   During DIME Drop 
Days, the team representatives will conduct their experiment in the 
NASA 2.2 second drop tower, analyze their data, tour NASA facilities, 
and participate in workshops.   
The Troy High School team was one of the four nationally selected and 
will trave l to Cleveland March 21  - 25, 2011,  to perform their 
experiments.  They are here tonight with their teacher, John Morrison, 
to share their project. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: This is a report. 
Troy School District Science Coordinator, Jennifer Gottlieb , will provide this

A.  CONSIDERATION:  INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT 
The District was approached by Avondale School District to provide 
administrative consulting services to Avondale.  It is appropriate for the Board to 
consider a resolution regarding these contracted services.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
Moved by:   
 Supported by: 
District to enter into an agreement with Avondale Sc hool District to provide 
certain management, consulting and su pervisory services relative to the 
administrative services and function of Avondale School District for a one year 
period starting July 1, 2010 and ending J une 30, 2011, with an option to renew; 
and further authorizes the Superintendent to enter into an agreement on behalf 
of the District.  A copy of the agreement will be attached to the Minutes of this 
meeting.   
Ayes: 
Nays: 
151
Be it therefore resolved that 
the Troy Board of Education authorizes the 
AGREEMENT 
FOR 
ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES CONSULTANTS 
 This AGREEMENT FOR ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES CONSULTANTS (the “Agreement”) 
is made as of the 1 st day of July, 2010 (the “Effective Date”), by and between AVONDALE SCHOOL 
DISTRICT, a Mi chigan general powers school district, whose address is 2940 Waukegan Street, Auburn 
Hills, Michigan 48326  (“Avondale”), and TROY SCHOOL DISTRICT , a Michigan general powers 
school district, whose address is 4400 Livernois, Troy, Michigan 48098  (“Troy”).  E ach a “Party” and 
collectively the “Parties.” 
RECITALS 
 WHEREAS, pursuant to Sections 11a(3) and (4) of the School Code MCL 380.1 et seq., a general 
powers school district has the authority to exercise a power incidental or appropriate to the performance o f 
any function related to the operation of the school district in the interests of public elementary and secondary 
education in the school district, and to enter into agreements or cooperative arrangements with other entities, 
public or private, or join organizations as part of performing the functions of the school district; and 
 WHEREAS, Avondale desires to engage Troy to receive certain management, consulting and 
supervisory services relative to the administrative services and functions of Avondale in accordance with the 
terms and conditions of this Agreement; and 
 WHEREAS, Troy desires to perform the management, consulting and supervisory services in 
accordance with the terms and conditions of this Agreement 
 NOW THEREFORE, IT IS HEREBY AGREED BETWEEN THE PARTIES AS FOLLOWS: 
I. ENGAGEMENT AND SCOPE OF SERVICES 
A. Consultants.  Avondale hereby engages Troy and Troy accepts such engagement to provide 
certain Troy employees to Avondale to provide the Services set forth in this Agreement for 
the consideration and upon the terms and conditions set forth herein.  The Parties agree that 
the following Troy employees will be provided to Avondale to perform the Services:  Mr. 
Jasen Witt (the “Human Resources Services Consultant”); Mr. Mark Rajter and Mr. Frank 
Lams (together, the “Business Management Consultant”); and Mr. Dominick Asaro (the 
“Transportation Consultant”), as well as other consultants as agreed upon by the Parties in 
writing (collectively the “Consultants”).  The Consultants are employed by Troy under a 
twelve (12) month annual work schedule and it is the intent of the Parties that Troy will make 
the Consultants available to perform the services defined below (collectively the “Services”). 
B. Human Resources Consulting Services .  Troy agrees to make the  Human Resources 
Consultant, and other individuals agreed to by the Parties in writing, available to Avondale to 
perform various human resources consulting services as requested by Avondale, which may 
include, but are not limited to: labor relations and hi ring consultation; benefits and 
compensation consultation; employee certification consultation; recordkeeping/reporting 
consultation; and attendance at Avondale Board of Education meetings as necessary 
(hereinafter the “Human Resources Consulting Services” ).  The Human Resources 
6462446.3 28938/136783 
- 1 - 
152
Consulting Services are more specifically described in Exhibit A , attached hereto and 
incorporated herein by reference.  It is agreed by the Parties that the Human Resources 
Services Consultant, Mr. Jasen Witt, shall not have the authority to act as an agent of 
Avondale to execute documents/reports unless authorized by the Superintendent of Avondale    
The Human Resources Consultant, including any other individuals agreed to by the parties, 
will establish a regular schedule to be on-site within Avondale, as agreed by the parties, and 
shall be available to Avondale for the provision of Human Resources Consulting Services in 
a reasonable and customary manner.   Given the periodic nature of some Human Resources 
Consulting Services (e.g. c ollective bargaining, labor relations, employee hiring, etc.), the 
parties recognize this schedule may need to be altered. 
C. Business Management Consulting Services .  Troy agrees to make the Business Services 
Consultant, and other individuals agreed to by the Parties in writing, available to Avondale to 
perform various business management consulting services as requested by Avondale, which 
may include, but are not limited to: consultation related to payroll, accounts payable and 
receivable; purchasing, and pupil accounting; consultation related to budget development and 
forecasting; taxes and other financial issues of Avondale; consultation related to cash flow, 
investments and borrowings; consultation related to annual reports required by federal and 
state laws; consultation related to annual audits and risk management; and attendance at 
Avondale Board of Education meetings as necessary (hereinafter the “Business Management 
Consulting Services”).  The Business Management Consulting Services are more specifically 
described in Exhibit B, attached hereto and incorporated herein by reference.  It is agreed by 
the Parties that the Business Management Services Consultant, Mr. Mark Rajter and Mr. 
Frank Lams, shall not have the authority to act as an agent of Avond ale to execute 
documents/reports unless authorized by the Superintendent of Avondale  The Business 
Management Consultant, including any other individuals agreed to by the parties, shall be 
available to Avondale approximately 40% of a full time equivalent b asis for both the 
Business Management Consulting Services and the Bond Project Consulting Services 
described below.  
D. Transportation Consulting Services.  Troy agrees to make the Transportation Consultant, and 
other individuals agreed to by the Parties in writing, available to Avondale to perform various 
transportation consulting services as requested by Avondale, which may include, but are not 
limited to: consultation related to routing and dispatching; consultation related to 
computerized routing softwa re applications; consultation related to transportation policies 
and procedures; consultation regarding bus maintenance and annual inspections; consultation 
related to new school bus acquisitions; consultation related to bus driver routing assignments; 
consultation related to student issues arising from school bus transportation; and attendance 
at Avondale Board of Education meetings as necessary (hereinafter the “Transportation 
Consulting Services”).  The Transportation Consulting Services are more specifi cally 
described in Exhibit C, attached hereto and incorporated herein by reference.  It is agreed by 
the Parties that the Transportation Services Consultant, Mr. Dominick Asaro, shall not have 
the authority to act as an agent of Avondale to execute documen ts/reports unless authorized 
by the Superintendent of Avondale. The Transportation Consultant, including any other 
6462446.3 28938/136783 
- 2 - 
153
individuals agreed to by the parties, shall be available to Avondale approximately 50% of a 
full time equivalent basis for the Transportation Consulting Services. 
E. Bond Project Consulting Services.  Troy agrees to make the Business Services Consultant, 
and other individuals agreed to by the Parties in writing, available to Avondale to perform 
various bond project consulting services as requested by Avondale, which may include, but 
are not limited to: consultation regarding the preparation of financial projections relating to 
the bond project; general oversight of the construction manager and architect as necessary or 
requested by Avondale; attendance at meetings with the construction manager and architect 
as necessary or requested by Avondale; consultation regarding the oversight of the 
construction fund and debt service retirement fund of the bond project; preparation of reports 
for the Avondale Board of Education regarding the bond project; and attendance at Avondale 
Board of Education meetings as necessary (hereinafter the “Bond Project Consulting 
Services”).  The Bond Project Consulting Services are more specifically described in Exhibit 
B, attached hereto and incorporated herein by reference.  It is agreed by the Parties that Troy 
personnel performing the Bond Project Consulting Services shall not have the authority to act 
as an agent of Avondale to execute documents/reports unless authorize d by the 
Superintendent of Avondale. 
F. Additional Services.  The Parties agree that they may expand the scope of the Services upon 
the mutual written agreement of both Parties.  Furthermore, Avondale may provide certain 
consulting services to Troy as mutually agreed upon by the Parties in writing.  Any mutually 
agreed upon expansion of the Services shall be set forth in a written amendment to this 
Agreement, executed by both Parties, and shall take effect as of the effective date of the 
written amendment. 
II. COMPENSATION 
A. Rate.  The Parties agree that Troy shall be reimbursed for the Services rendered by the 
Consultants under the formula as mutually agreed to by the Parties in writing, provided, 
however, that the total fees for the Services shall not exc eed two hundred thousand and 
00/100 Dollars ($200,000.00) annually.   
B. Rate Adjustment.  The aforementioned rates for the Services may be adjusted after the Initial 
Term if this Agreement is extended, or at intervals otherwise agreed to by the Parties.  Any 
such agreement and adjustment to the rates shall be mutually agreed upon by the Parties and 
set forth in writing.  Minimally, the Parties agree to use their best efforts to mutually agree 
upon on any rate adjustment minimally by March 1, and such agree d upon rate adjustment 
shall go into effect as of July 1 of following year. 
C. Reimbursement for Mileage and Travel.  For reasonable mileage incurred by the Consultants 
in rendering the Services, if agreed upon by Avondale, Avondale agrees to reimburse Troy, at 
the Internal Revenue Service (“IRS”) rates.  If the Consultants are required to travel 
(including the attendance at conferences on behalf of Avondale) to perform the Services and 
incur costs other than mileage, Avondale will reimburse Troy for all re asonable travel costs 
approved in advance by Avondale, but pursuant to Troy reimbursement policy, a copy of 
which will be provided to Avondale upon request.  If the Consultant is required to attend any 
6462446.3 28938/136783 
- 3 - 
154
professional development as part of, or in order to, e ffectively perform the Services, it is 
agreed by the Parties that the costs for such professional development will be split equally 
amongst Avondale and Troy. 
III. INVOICING AND PAYMENT 
Troy shall submit invoices to Avondale, detailing any Services render ed and costs incurred, on or 
before January 1 and June 30 of each fiscal year, together with all supporting documentation and 
records as requested by Avondale.  Avondale shall make payment of undisputed amounts no later 
than February 1 and August 1, respec tively, of each fiscal year.  Disputes regarding amounts 
contained in any invoice will be communicated to Troy by Avondale, in writing, within fifteen (15) 
business days of the receipt of the disputed invoice.  Payments of disputed amounts will be delayed 
unless Troy is able to resolve the matter to Avondale’s satisfaction within fifteen (15) business days 
prior to payment due date.  Avondale will not be assessed any late payment penalties, fines or charges 
for disputed amounts not timely paid due to Troy’s  failure to timely resolve the matter as set forth 
above. 
IV. PERFORMANCE 
Avondale’s Superintendent shall serve as Avondale’s liaison for this Agreement and the Services.  
Troy agrees that the Consultants shall perform all the Services in a professional an d workmanlike 
manner in accordance with all applicable federal, state and local laws, rules, regulations and 
ordinances, as well as applicable written policies and procedures of Avondale. The parties agree that 
in the event that both Avondale and Troy have  an issue that arises at the same time which requires 
the services and expertise of any Consultant, the issue which presents the most immediate and serious 
safety or health or monetary concern shall be addressed first, in the sole discretion of the Consultant, 
who shall be advised of this provision. 
V. COMMUNICATION 
The parties shall meet and confer on a mutually agreeable date and time on no less than a quarterly 
basis to discuss the performance of the Consultants.  Avondale shall notify Troy of any scheduling, 
performance or conduct issue related to the services provided under this Agreement within two (2) 
days of receiving notice of the issue. All communications to Avondale shall be provided to its 
Superintendent, or his/her designee.  All communications  to Troy shall be provided to its 
Superintendent, or his/her designee. 
VI. TERM AND TERMINATION 
This Agreement shall commence on July 1, 2010 and shall expire on June 30, 2011 (the “Initial 
Term”).  This Agreement may be renewed by the Parties for periods of one (1) year, commencing on 
July 1, 2011 and annually thereafter (each a “Renewal Term”), provided that the Parties agree upon 
such extension in writing at least 60 days prior to the date of expiration of the current term.  The 
Parties mutually agree that this Agreement may be terminated, in whole or in part, by either Party for 
“Cause” if a Party fails to perform, or cure its performance of, its obligations under this Agreement 
6462446.3 28938/136783 
- 4 - 
155
within thirty (30) days of its receipt of written notice from the other Par ty stating such breach/non-
performance.  Notwithstanding the foregoing, either Party may terminate this Agreement, in whole or 
in part, for any reason, with or without cause, upon sixty (60) days prior written notice.  Furthermore, 
if a Consultant is no lo nger available to provide the Services, either party may terminate this 
Agreement, or any portion thereof, immediately.  In the event of termination, Avondale shall only be 
liable for payment for Services actually rendered and reimbursements previously approved.  Notice 
shall be delivered by the terminating Party via return receipt, certified US Mail or delivered in person 
to the Superintendent of the other Party. 
VII. RELATIONSHIP OF THE PARTIES 
The relationship between Avondale and Troy (including the Consultants) shall be that of independent 
contracting parties.  The Consultants in performing their duties under this Agreement shall, except as 
otherwise specified in this Agreement, be subject only to the oversight and direction of the Avondale 
superintendent and no other employee of Avondale, and shall abide by the terms of this Agreement, 
as well as the policies and procedures of Avondale.  Moreover, Troy is the Employer of Record with 
respect to the Consultants under this Agreement and shall be responsibl e for costs of employment 
(salary, taxes, fees, fringe and/or retirement benefits, insurance, worker’s disability compensation, 
etc.) relative to each Consultant.  This Agreement shall not be construed as authority for any Party to 
act for another Party in  any agency or other capacity or to make commitments of any kind for the 
account of, or on behalf of, another Party, except to the extent, and for the purposes, expressly 
provided for and set forth herein, and no partnership or joint venture is created hereby.  It is expressly 
agreed that neither Troy nor the Consultant are entitled to participate in any plans, arrangements, or 
distributions by Avondale pertaining to or in connection with any fringe, pension, bonus, profit 
sharing, or similar benefits, or any medical, dental, life or disability insurance plans.   
VIII. RESERVATION OF RIGHTS 
This Agreement does not, and is not intended to impair, divest, delegate, or contravene any 
constitutional, statutory, and/or other legal right, privilege, power, obligat ion, duty or immunity of 
each Party and shall not be construed to  waive the defense of governmental immunity held by any 
Party to this Agreement. 
IX. RECORDS 
All records, forms and supplies or any reproduced copies provided and furnished by Avondale to 
Troy or the Consultants or obtained by Troy or the Consultants during the course of rendering the 
Services to Avondale shall always remain the property of Avondale and shall be returned to 
Avondale on demand, or upon the expiration or termination of this Agre ement.  All records and 
related documents prepared by the Consultants or otherwise created in connection with the rendering 
of Services to Avondale shall be prepared in accordance with applicable law and the practices and 
procedures of Avondale and shall b e the property of Avondale.  Troy and the Consultants shall 
maintain records of any obligations performed, and any payments received or costs incurred under 
this Agreement.  Such records shall be kept in accordance with Generally Accepted Accounting 
Principles, and shall be made available to Avondale upon reasonable notice.  Except as required by 
its duties to Avondale or by law, Troy agrees that it, the Consultants and its agents will never, during 
the Term of this Agreement or at any time subsequent to th e expiration or termination of this 
6462446.3 28938/136783 
- 5 - 
156
Agreement, directly or indirectly use or disclose any confidential information of Avondale without 
the prior written consent of Avondale’s Superintendent.  All records must be retained in compliance 
with Bulletin 522.  Furthermore, Troy and its Consultants shall comply with all applicable provisions 
of the Family Educational Rights and Privacy Act (“FERPA”) in performing the Services 
X. TENURE DISCLAIMER 
Troy acknowledges and agrees that, notwithstanding anything to the contrary, any person performing 
Services under this Agreement shall not acquire teacher tenure, administrative tenure, or any other 
tenure rights in any capacity whatsoever, regardless of where such Services are performed. 
XI. OTHER ACTIVITIES 
Troy shall remain free to engage in other independent contracting activities, provided, however, that 
Troy and the Consultants shall remain available to perform the Services under this Agreement in in 
accordance with State standards of performance for school administrators performing such Services 
and shall refrain from engaging in any activities which are inconsistent with, which interfere with, or 
which are in conflict with any of the terms of this Agreement. 
XII. FINGERPRINTING AND BACKGROUND CHECK 
Troy acknowledges  and agrees that the Consultants and any of its agents, employees or 
representatives of Troy who will be on Avondale premises regularly and continuously to perform the 
Services will be fingerprinted and subjected to criminal history and background checks through the 
Michigan State Police and Federal Bureau of Investigation, as detailed in Public Act 680 of 2006, as 
amended, prior to commencing any Services under this Agreement by presenting themselves, or any 
of its agents, employees or representatives, for  proper fingerprinting and criminal backgrounds 
checks, as directed by Avondale, or provide written notification to Avondale that the Consultants and 
other Troy personnel have previously completed fingerprinting and a criminal history and 
background check in connection with contracting or working for Troy, another school district, 
intermediate school district, public school academy or nonpublic school (each an “Agency”), and 
consent to the sharing or transferring of the appropriate fingerprinting and crimin al history 
background report from the other Agency.  Additionally, Troy represents and warrants to Avondale 
that it will at all times during the Initial Term, or any Renewal Term(s) of this Agreement be in 
compliance with the provisions of Michigan Public Act 680 of 2006, as amended, including, but not 
limited to, reporting to Avondale within 3 business days of when any Consultant or any Troy agents, 
employees or representatives who will regularly and continuously be on Avondale premises to 
perform the Serv ices, is/are charged with a crime listed in Section 1535a(1) or 1539b(1) of the 
Revised School Code, being MCL 380.1535a(1) and 380.1539b(1), or a substantially similar law, 
and to immediately report to Avondale if that person is subsequently convicted, plead guilty or plead 
no contest to that crime. Troy shall be responsible for all costs and expenses associated with the 
above-required fingerprinting and background checks.  Troy shall supply all necessary data and 
information, as requested by Avondale, to enable Avondale to properly submit the Consultants or any 
other Troy employees and agents for inclusion in the State of Michigan Department of Education’s 
list of “registered educational personnel” as may be required.

XIII. WORKING FACILITIES 
The Consultants may utilize those premises and facilities of Avondale in rendering Services pursuant

XIV. NON-EXCLUSIVE SERVICES 
Avondale does not agree to use Troy exclusively to perform the Services and is free to engage other 
parties to perform similar services as required under this Agreement.

XV. MISCELLANEOUS 
A. Entire Agreement.  This Agreement sets forth all the covenants, agreements, stipulations, 
promises, conditions and understandings of the parties to the Conso rtium concerning the 
activities and Services contemplated herein.  No Party, including but not limited to its 
respective Board members, employees, attorneys, consultants, advisors, agents, 
representatives or students, have made any covenant, agreement, sti pulation, promise, 
condition or understanding, warranty or representation, either oral or written, other than set 
forth herein. 
B. Amendment.  This Agreement shall not be modified, altered or amended except by written 
agreement duly executed by all parties to this Agreement in accordance with the terms hereof. 
C. Invalidity of Particular Provision.  The invalidity of any article, section, subsection, clause or 
provision of this Agreement shall not affect the validity of the remaining sections, 
subsections, clauses or provisions hereof which remain valid and be enforced to the fullest 
extent permitted by law. 
D. Captions.  The captions in this Agreement are inserted only as a matter of convenience and  
for reference and in no way define, limit, enlarge or des cribe the scope or intent of this 
Agreement nor in any way shall affect this Agreement or the construction of any provision 
hereof. 
E. Waivers.  A Party may not waive any default, condition, promise, obligation or requirement 
applicable to the other party hereunder, unless such waiver is in writing signed by an 
authorized representative of such Party and expressly stated to constitute such waiver.  Such 
waiver shall only apply to the extent given and shall not be deemed or construed to waive any 
such or oth er default, condition, promise, obligation or requirement in any past or future 
instance.  No failure by a Party to insist upon strict performance of any covenant, agreement, 
term, or condition of this Agreement, or to the exercise any right or remedy in t he event of 
default, shall constitute a waiver of any such default of such covenant, agreement, term or 
condition. 
F. Governing Law.  This Agreement shall be governed by, construed and enforced in accordance 
with, the laws of the State of Michigan, and Par ties consent to the jurisdiction and venue of 
the appropriate Oakland County Court. 
G. Successors and Assigns.  The covenants, conditions and agreements in this Agreement shall 
be binding upon and inure to the benefit of each Party, their respective legal representatives, 
successors and assigns. 
H. Counterparts.  This Agreement may be executed in any number of counterparts, each of 
which shall be an original, but all such counterparts shall together constitute one and the 
same instrument.

MEMORANDUM 
To: Frank Lams, Purchasing Supervisor  
From: Scott Bryan, Director of Technology  
Date: January 11, 2011 
Re: Bid 9701 web hosting service 
In compliance with federal E-Rate bidding practices and in order to secure an estimated 38% discount , we 
released a bid for web hosting service for the district on November 11, 2010.  Bids were opened January 5, 
2011.  Four bid packets that complied with the RFP were received; other bid responses via email or other 
non-compliant methods were rejected.  
I have analyzed the bids from the two apparent lowest solu tions – THiNQ Ed and Edline.  Upon further 
review, the bid from THiNQ Ed fails to meet all bid specifications – specifically the requirement to be 100% 
compatible with GradeQuick.  The THiNQ Ed response indicates that it does work with the GradeQuick 
desktop edition, but not GradeQuick Web, which is proprietary.  The District migrated exclusively to 
GradeQuick Web two years ago, therefore the THiNQ  Ed solution does not meet District standards.  The 
next lowest bid from Edline is fully compliant with all bid  specifications. 
Based on this analysis, I recommend award of a one year contract with Edline in the amount of $29,436 to 
commence July 1, 2011 per E-Rate regulations.  The E-Rate timeframe requires forms awarding the bid to be 
completed now in order to meet eligibility rules for the federal discount.  
This item required Board of Education action; p lease place this on the January 18, 2011 agenda.  Thank you 
for your attention to this matter.

          RE:      Bid Award #9699

4420 Livernois Phone No. 248-823-5090 
Troy, Michigan 48098 Fax No.  248-823-5050 
MEMORANDUM 
To: Frank Lams, Purchasing Supervisor  
From: Scott Bryan, Director of Technology  
Date: January 11, 2011 
Re: Bid 9699 PRI Local and Long Distance service  
In compliance with federal E-Rate bidding practices and in order to secure an estimated 38% discount on 
telephone services, we released a bid for PRI/long distance telephone service for the district on November 
11, 2010.  Bids were opened January 5, 2011.  Four bid packets were received; the packet from Cavalier 
Telecom included two different bid price responses.  The packet from PAETEC Fairpor t NY had conflicting 
data on the bid form vs. the backup documentation and was rejected after review with the vendor.  Therefore, 
four bid responses were considered:  Cavalier Telecom measured rate, Cavalier Telecom flat rate, PAETEC 
Tampa, and Telcom Worldwide. 
The bid responses include a fixed monthly charge for each circuit plus a variable usage rate per minute for 
calling patterns.  I have created an evaluation matrix that used actual numbers of phone calls and per minute 
usage for the district from October 2010.  Traditionally October calling is a high water mark for telephone 
usage, so it is a reasonable “worst case scenario” to project telephone expenses.  Based on this, the attached 
spreadsheet projects that the response from PAETEC Tampa is the “wo rst case” low bid response.  Please 
note that, based on the nature of the bid responses, lower usage, i.e., fewer local calls or less long distance 
minutes usage simply increases the value of the PAETEC solution over other options.  
There were some clerical errors in the PAETEC Tampa bid form – specifically, they entered the monthly 
cost for all 4 circuits instead of each individual circuit.  However, the backup documentation/sample contract 
provided in the bid response permitted for an easy evaluation and correction, so although the initial “as read” 
bid opening pricing appears that this bid is highest by several orders of magnitude, the error is easily 
understood using documentation provided within the bid response.  Due to the overall significant cost 
savings over the life of the contract, I recommend exercising the District’s right to waive minor irregularities 
in the bid process – the vendor has not changed their response in any way as a result of my review.  
Based on the above analysis, I recommend that  a three year contract for PRI, local and long distance 
telecommunications services be awarded to PAETEC Tampa, contract to commence July 1, 2011 per E -Rate 
rules.  The E-Rate timeframe requires forms awarding the bid to be completed now in order to meet 
eligibility rules for the federal discount.  
Please place this on the January 18, 2011 agenda.  Thank you for your attention to this matter.
